this area equals 1, mathematically, ^0oFUBpAO

p(  < X <  +d ) ^nGKE9ZKp

So, in this example, what is the probability, if we did a random sample of the cars, pick a car at random, and ask how much time it take to drive between A and B.
What's the probability that the time that car took was bigger than x and smaller than X+dx.

That's the formal definition of the probability density function,  that for each little segment (infinitely small), if you take the base (d ) and multiply with the height (f    ) tells you the probability that your random variable is in that range. ^GoXBEoV8

It's used for a lot of cases, but why?
Because of the central limit theorem (c.l.t). ^MtgzEMVF
